## Rulings
- Ruling R1: Use pnpm 10 / Python 3.13 locally; CI pins pnpm 10 and Python 3.12+3.13 matrix — plan says "or later" — cost if wrong: trivial version bump.
- Ruling R2: Workspace dir excluded via .git/info/exclude, not .gitignore — keeps repo clean — cost: none.
- Ruling R3: Task 11 `createSource(projectId, draft, files, opts: { provenance, fieldEvidence?: FieldEvidence[], verification?: Record<string,FieldVerification>, availability?: Availability })`; repo runs `recheckSourceFile` only from T16 onward (T11 stores what it's given) — cost: small signature change later.
- Ruling R4: Task 3 adds `identifiers.platform?: 'youtube'|'instagram'|'tiktok'|'other'` — needed for dedupe — cost: trivial.
- Ruling R5: Task 12 `review_items(id, project_id, batch_id NULL, source_id NULL, kind 'import'|'verification', status 'pending'|'resolved', payload_json, created_at)`.
- Ruling R6: Task 12 `sources` table has columns `platform, platform_post_id, doi, isbn13, sha256s_json` with indexes for duplicate lookups; `findSourceBy({platform, postId} | {doi} | {isbn} | {sha256})` exported from index/search.ts.
- Ruling R7: Task 15 creates `scripts/fetch-fixtures.mjs` (LibriSpeech sample + one CC0 DNG from raw.pixls.us, sha256-pinned); Task 21 extends it with CR3 — cost: none.
- Ruling R8: Task 16 defines `LookupEvidence = { provider: string, matchedIdentifier?: string, title?: string, creators?: Creator[], issued?: string }` in verify/evidence.ts.
- Ruling R9: Task 18 records importBatchId on every source it creates (ImportBatch kind 'device'→ add kind 'profile-capture'); the undo-of-profile-batch test moves to Task 23 — cost: undo coverage arrives 5 tasks later.

- Ruling R33 (Task 13 fixes): sourceRepo/destRepo split; destination always opened for hint+audit. Residency classified on the realpath of the nearest existing ancestor AND the typed path, stricter wins. Uncertain classification (failed mount probe, unrecognised location) → planMove evaluates every plausible kind (device, removable, network) and denies if any is denied. ~/Library/CloudStorage/<X> → cloud_folder:<x>; UNC → network; OneDrive env vars honoured; Linux /media,/run/media,/mnt/* → removable. Project folder without readable project.json but with governance.yaml or Sources → MoveNotAllowedError. After switch: old root gets MOVED.json; openLibrary on a folder with MOVED.json → read-only 'moved'; findLibrary ranks/flags moved copies; split mode marks moved project folders (repo treats them read-only). Pending old-copy deletions persisted in appConfigDir (survive restart; confirm again). Journal 'switching' phase + idempotent completeMove. Preflight refuses case-fold/NFC name collisions. deleteOldCopy holds the lock; split-mode allow-list; excluded projects get 'library.split' audit event.
- Ruling R34: Task 14 (findLibrary) must flag folders containing MOVED.json and rank them below live copies (carry into Task 14 dispatch).

- Ruling R37: Task 16 — evidence counts toward a field only if its value agrees with the current draft value; lookups count only if matchedIdentifier matches a draft identifier (or none given and value agrees); AI quotes must contain the value (normalized, token-bounded; year for dates) and be ≥3 tokens (≥4 CJK chars); grounding uses token-boundary matching with min length (Latin titles ≥2 tokens or ≥8 chars; family names ≥3 chars else require a second independent source; CJK ≥2 chars); user-confirmed only when the user evidence value agrees with the draft value.
- Ruling R38: Task 16 round 2 — creators agree only if the full ordered list of family names (or handles/literal-derived family) matches; user-confirmed requires strict agreement (normalized-equal titles, full creator list, identical issued string) and is dropped when no user evidence exists; draft value goes in new optional FieldVerification.current (core model), not values[] with kind 'user'; draft-vs-evidence /issued stays exact (online-first vs print flagged for review — intended).
- Ruling R39: Task 16 round 3 — literal names compared whole (comma literal → family before comma; no-comma literal matches only a structured name whose 'given family' or 'family given' equals it); grounding of a literal needs the whole phrase; strict user-confirmed: identifier lists set-equal (canonical), every title variant equal, creators equal on family+given+literal+handle; integrity checks (ISBN checksum, DOI) run before user-confirmed and override it; draft-vs-evidence: title near-match (sim ≥0.9 but not normalized-equal) → unverified 'differs slightly'; draft date more precise than evidence → unverified; AI values obey the same minimum-length grounding rule; text fields compared case/punctuation-insensitively (normalizeTitle), ISSN uppercase-X canonical, URL host lowercased path case-sensitive.

Task 23 behaviour review (a06cd0f): Needs fixes. Ruling R61: (1) undo deletes a batch-created tag/collection only if no surviving source uses it and (collections) it has no user-added children; else keep it and note in undo result; (2) merge undo reverts only what the merge added (journal added asset ids + added field paths); if the target changed since (audit/changelog after the merge), revert only untouched added fields, keep user edits, and report "kept"; batches undone out of order must not trash another batch's assets; (3) batch id joins the running set synchronously before any await; batch-number allocation serialised (in-process mutex + exclusive mkdir of batch-NNNN); (4) R44: forget only post ids whose sources undo actually removed; (5) watchdog clock starts after worker 'online' + warm-up; (M) vetter treats [] and [^] as complete classes; decideReview only for kind 'import'; batchReport read-only on view (audit event only at batch end); journal outcome update on review decisions (append a 'reviewed' line); resume guard: source with same importBatchId+item key = done; dedupe sees sources added during the batch (refresh exact keys per group from the index); ZIP duplicate-name entries → item failed with "duplicate name in archive", bomb → size message; link refusal without governance.yaml only when encryption required (missing governance = default policy).

If store is denied (e.g. encryption off + coarsen), the item fails with the engine's reason. Proxy generation must verify the output's GPS (re-read EXIF of the proxy) matches the mode before recording it. - Ruling R96 (Task 30 service security + carry-overs): listen on 127.0.0.1 only; bearer token compared in constant time; AO_SERVICE_TOKEN read once then deleted from env; Host header must be 127.0.0.1:<port> or localhost:<port> (DNS-rebinding defence); requests with an Origin header refused unless it is the desktop shell's origin (tauri://localhost / http://tauri.localhost) or the extension's chrome-extension:// id paired; no CORS wildcard; tokens never accepted in query strings (SSE via fetch streaming with Authorization); extension token scoped to /capture/browser + GET /projects only; pairing: 6-digit code from crypto RNG, single use, 5 min, max 5 attempts then invalidated, rate-limited; body size limit (1 MB JSON default; larger only where needed); Fastify logger redacts authorization, secret, passphrase, recoveryCode, apiKey; errors scrubbed of absolute paths except routes whose response is the user's own chosen path; every mutating route goes through the governed library APIs (no direct fs). The original plan remains the specification of Tasks 34 and 37, which are split (34a–34f, 37a–37i); new tasks are numbered from 38. The skill's `task-brief` script reads the original plan only; PLAN.md tasks get hand-written briefs quoting the rulings in their `[ledger: …]` tag. — cost if wrong: re-planning time.
- Ruling R122 (owner, 2026-11-11): scope is Phase 1 in detail, later phases in outline. Platforms: macOS first, Apple Silicon and Intel, as two downloads unless Task 37e shows one combined app is practical; Windows after Phase 1. The owner creates an Apple Developer account for signing. — cost if wrong: Intel packaging may prove impractical for the worker's speech and image libraries; 37e finds out before 37f depends on it.
- Ruling R123 (owner): `main` was created at 0ad6506 and is the approved state; it changes only with the owner's approval. Work happens on the session's working branch, pushed after every completed task. The `claude/eager-sagan-ndp8ku*` branches are frozen. — cost if wrong: none to the product.
- Ruling R124 (owner; closes the open decision in R87): citeproc-js is used under CPAL-1.0. The project's own licence is not chosen yet; AGPL-3.0 is the likely later choice and stays possible (citeproc-js is dual-licensed, and the owner holds the rights in the project's own code). No project LICENSE file is added and nothing is published until the owner decides (gate in Task 56). What CPAL requires of the app is recorded from the licence text by Task 40. — cost if wrong: a missing attribution display or notice at first release; re-checked at Task 56.
- Ruling R125 (owner): making a backup, by hand or by the daily schedule, is evaluated by the policy engine and audited; a refused scheduled backup raises `backup-failed` (R91, R92: never silent). Removing old backups is audited by file name and stays a plain delete (R91). Backups stay in `<Library>/Backups/` under the manifest so destruction under retention can find and certify them (R90 d, R92, R94). A second backup location is not built in Phase 1; the owner decides it when sync is designed (Phase 5). — cost if wrong: until then a backup does not survive losing the drive that holds the library; the owner was told to copy the library folder elsewhere by hand meanwhile.
- Ruling R126 (owner; replaces the bundling of ffmpeg, tesseract and ghostscript in the original Task 37): on first use the app asks which optional tools the researcher needs and downloads only those. ADR 0009 stays in force: yt-dlp is the latest release, checksum-verified, in the tools folder, updated in place; gallery-dl is pinned inside the worker; exiftool is bundled and required. A feature whose tool is missing says so; without ffmpeg, located media stays "proxy pending" and out of exports (R83, R90 c). — cost if wrong: first use needs a network connection.
- Ruling R127 (process): fix rounds as the skill sets them: rounds 1–3 resume the implementer, rounds 4–5 a fresh implementer on a more capable tier, then the controller adjudicates and ledgers what is left. Small documentation, CI and test-expectation changes are reviewed by the controller reading the diff and running the named checks, not by a reviewer sub-agent; everything else gets an adversarial reviewer. — why: the owner's rule is that the controller's own usage is the scarce resource — cost if wrong: a small change escapes an adversarial look until the final whole-branch review (Task 54).
- Ruling R128 (Task 38): the stale expectation in `apps/cli/src/cli.test.ts` (dry-run `outsideFiles`) is corrected in the test, not the code: the `file` field was added on purpose (R118 e), the change is additive, and `apps/cli/README.md` does not document that JSON. Process note: two reviewers used the same probe file name and one overwrote the other's; probe names carry the reviewer's task id from now on (`*.probe41a.test.ts`).
- Ruling R131 (pending writes; sharpens R26, R27, R118 g after 41a C1, I1 and 41b2 I3): (a) an `unverifiable` marker offers neither "keep" nor "roll back"; like an invalid marker, nothing it names is touched. (b) "Roll back" is offered and performed only when the marker is valid, the change is not committed, no event later than the marker's start exists (audit count at the recorded offset equals the marker's `auditSeqBefore`; the anchor is not past the start), and every recorded append offset lands on a line boundary; any failed check refuses with a plain reason and changes nothing. (c) The resolve is durable before it acts: the choice is recorded with the marker, the action is idempotent, the marker goes only after the `txn.resolve` audit event is written, and a leftover recorded choice is completed (and audited) on the next open. (d) A resolve never moves the device anchor past a mismatch. (e) For a marker with no choice on offer the dialog says the app cannot confirm whether the change was finished and will change nothing itself, names what the marker lists, and points to restoring the project from a backup. — why: acting on offsets the audit record cannot back destroys committed history, which is worse than a blocked project — cost if wrong: a researcher whose audit record is damaged stays blocked on that project until a backup is restored; whether to add a deliberate, audited "set aside" for such markers is an owner decision, raised at the next checkpoint.

Being diagnosed; no green run on the fixed build yet (Tasks 38 and 42 are verified locally only).
CI diagnosis (job 130605286691): GitHub's runner could not resolve `azure.archive.ubuntu.com` or `security.ubuntu.com`; `apt-get update` failed on DNS after 3 min 20 s and `apt-get install` then retried one file every 20 s with no download succeeding. An outage on GitHub's side, not a fault in the repository. Mitigation: the job now has a 45-minute limit and the apt step an 8-minute limit with bounded retries, so a stuck run fails fast instead of consuming build minutes. Until a run completes, Tasks 38 and 42 remain verified locally only.

Task 37e: complete (merged a3bbbbb): `docs/adr/0014-macos-tools.md`, status Proposed, with sources checked 2026-11-11 and a list of what could not be verified. Findings that change the plan: (1) the pinned worker libraries need macOS 15 (pikepdf 10.18 needs 15.0; av and onnxruntime need 14); (2) four libraries publish no Intel-Mac build at the current pins, so an Intel worker needs a second, older pin set (onnxruntime 1.23.2; pikepdf 10.13.0.post1, which forces ocrmypdf ≤ 17.12.1; rawpy 0.25.1; cryptography 48.0.1) and PyInstaller cannot make one combined app; (3) ghostscript is not needed (ocrmypdf 17 rasterises with pypdfium2, already a dependency); tesseract has no official Mac binaries and would be built and signed in our CI; (4) yt-dlp now needs a JavaScript runtime (deno by default) for full YouTube support, and the worker passes none — no test caught this because every test uses a stand-in for yt-dlp; (5) exiftool runs under the system Perl, which Apple has said may be removed; (6) GitHub runner labels today: Apple Silicon `macos-15`, Intel `macos-15-intel`; `macos-14` was retired on 2 November 2026.
- Ruling R133 (Mac packaging, from ADR 0014; the two points marked "owner" wait for the owner's confirmation and are assumed meanwhile): two downloads, Apple Silicon built on `macos-15` and Intel on `macos-15-intel`, no combined app; minimum macOS 15.0 for both (owner); the Intel download is kept, on its own older pin set, built and tested separately (owner: it costs older RAW-camera support, two months of OCR fixes and an older cryptography library on Intel, and ends if GitHub retires its Intel machines); ghostscript leaves the optional-tools list and the Mac build (Linux CI keeps it until the worker's tests are shown to pass without it); tesseract is built and signed in our CI and offered in the first-use dialogue; yt-dlp is offered together with the JavaScript runtime it needs, and the worker names that runtime to yt-dlp explicitly; a live, opt-in check with the real yt-dlp is added so this class of drift is caught. — cost if wrong: Mac packaging rework before the first release; nothing shipped yet depends on it.
Task 60 review (`.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/task-60-review.md`): Spec ✅; quality Needs fixes — 0 Critical, 2 Important, 10 Minor. I1: a lost response after the service created the project leaves the recovery code never shown, and a retry creates a second project with the same name. I2: the recovery-code step can be left before confirmation (Back, reload, tab close, a library-closed event). The "rules need encryption" refusal is matched by message text; the reviewer found the UI's own check agrees with the service in all 32 profile combinations and the researcher can still switch encryption on by hand if the text stops matching; a stable reason code from the service is the better fix later (minor, deferred). Fix round 1/5 sent to the same implementer.
- Ruling R134 (recovery code, Task 60): (a) when the outcome of a create request is unknown the dialog re-reads the project list and compares ids before any retry; if the project now exists it does not resend, says the recovery code could not be shown and cannot be shown again, and selects the project; if the re-read fails it does not resend blindly. (b) While an unconfirmed recovery code is on screen the dialog blocks leaving (unload guard, in-app navigation refused, service events do not unmount it). (c) A governed, audited "issue a new recovery code" action for an unlocked encrypted project is planned as Task 61 (library, service, UI): no such function exists today (`crypto/keys.ts` creates the recovery wrapping only at project creation), and a crash or power loss during the one-time display can always lose a code. — cost if wrong: until Task 61 a researcher who loses the code has only the passphrase; the dialog says so.
CI diagnosis, second pass (run 43049632507, diagnostic step): on GitHub's runner the three Ubuntu mirror names (`azure.archive.ubuntu.com`, `archive.ubuntu.com`, `security.ubuntu.com`) do not resolve; npm, PyPI, GitHub, the fixture hosts and the Playwright CDN all resolve and answer. GitHub's status page reports no incident. So the fault is in name resolution for ubuntu.com as seen from the runners, not in the repository and not a general network restriction. Workaround in `ci.yml`: when the mirror name does not resolve, the apt step points apt at `mirrors.edge.kernel.org` for that run (packages stay verified against Ubuntu's signing keys). The diagnostic step stays until a run is green, then is removed.

Task 60: fix round 1/5 re-review: I2 ADDRESSED; I1 NOT ADDRESSED on one path — in the "Check again" state Escape and Cancel close the dialog, and reopening and submitting sends a second create while the first project (recovery code never shown) is invisible because the library never re-reads its list. Fix round 2/5 sent: the library re-reads its list whenever the dialog closes; the unknown-outcome state cannot be dismissed silently; a same-name project needs explicit confirmation before a create is sent.
- Ruling R135 (Task 34a, media delivery; extends R96, amends the content-security policy of R105/R109): (1) Pictures (screenshots, image proxies, thumbnails) are read with the bearer token from an asset-content route and shown as blob URLs, revoked when no longer shown; no policy change is needed (`img-src blob:` is already allowed) and this matches how snapshots work (R101). (2) Audio and video are played through a media ticket: an authenticated call with the UI token (never the extension token) issues a ticket for exactly one asset of one unlocked project; the ticket is 256 random bits, lives only in the service's memory, is carried in the URL path (`/media/<ticket>`, no query string), allows only GET and HEAD with range requests, expires after 10 idle minutes with a hard limit of 8 hours, and is revoked when its project is locked, the library is closed or moved, or the service stops; at most 16 tickets are live at once. (3) The media route serves only assets whose recorded type is audio or video, with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Cache-Control: no-store` and an inline disposition, streams with decryption for encrypted projects without holding the file in memory, keeps the Host check, and never writes a ticket to logs or error messages. (4) The UI's content-security policy gains `media-src http://127.0.0.1:* http://localhost:* blob:`, the same reach `connect-src` already has, and nothing else changes in it. (5) The UI treats a ticket address as a secret: never shown as text, never logged, never stored, never put in the page address. Rejected for now: the desktop shell forwarding media requests and adding the token (keeps credentials out of addresses altogether, but cannot run in the browser end-to-end tests and puts request handling in the shell). — why: a media element cannot send an Authorization header, and loading a two-hour recording into memory as a blob is not acceptable — cost if wrong: a ticket leaked from the page lets a local program read that one recording until it expires; revisit with the shell-forwarding design if a reviewer shows the ticket can escape, or if the Mac web view mishandles cross-origin ranged media (checked by the WebKit end-to-end scenario in Task 34f).

Task 45 review (`.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/task-45-review.md`): Spec ✅; quality Needs fixes — 0 Critical, 3 Important, 9 Minor, all in test code. I1: blocked steps are skipped, so a fixed defect would stay silently skipped (the staleness check is opt-in). I2: the D4 and D2 fixme bodies do not match what "fixed" means. I3: `app.restart` leaks a service that is still starting when a test times out. No network use; nothing weakened. Reviewer's classification of the product defects: D1 real, Important; D4 product gap in the wizard and report, Important (the engine's linking of Zotero-linked files is deliberate, R72); D2 design ambiguity, Minor; D3 planned (34a/34d); D5 missing feature, contradicts SPEC 4.5, Minor; D6 unproven, likely harness or WebKit-on-Linux; D7 real, small. Fix round 1/5 sent to the same implementer.
- Ruling R136 (import wizard, defect D4; refines R72 for the wizard): "Copy into the library" copies Zotero's linked attachments that the researcher approved (R67), like any other file. They stay links only by an explicit, separate choice shown when the scan found linked attachments, never offered where the project's rules require encryption (R24). The import report always gives the number of files copied and the number left as links. The engine's default for callers that say nothing (the CLI) is unchanged and the CLI guide must say what it is. — why: the owner's stated default is copy, and a link is outside encryption and retention control — cost if wrong: importing a Zotero library with large linked files duplicates them; the scan summary shows the size before the researcher confirms.
- Ruling R137 (library list, defect D2): the "Needs review" badge on a library row means the source has an item waiting in the review queue and nothing else; any decision on the item clears it. Field-level verification (unverified, conflict, confirmed by you) is shown by the verification badges on the source screen (Task 34d); accepting a review item does not mark its fields confirmed, except fields the researcher edited there. — cost if wrong: a researcher may read a cleared row badge as "all metadata verified"; the source screen's badges say otherwise.
Task 34a: implemented (worktree branch `worktree-agent-ac849bfb2738941ce`, ad61439..0dd43ae, 7 commits; report `.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/task-34a-report.md`): media tickets as R135 rules, pictures by authenticated fetch (48 MiB limit), transcripts as segments (`flags: null` for a human edit), evidence and verification on source records, tests for capture-profile, transcribe, acknowledge and tools update, the `media-src` policy rule, shared types in `packages/shared/src/api/`. Service 131 pass; the 50 MB encrypted range read holds about 8 MB. Implementer's decisions: the media route requires the shell's Origin when one is sent, refuses navigations and any fetch destination other than audio, video, track or empty, refuses a Referer from another site, and serves clients that send no fetch metadata on the ticket alone; multiple ranges or If-Range → whole file with 200; the 17th ticket → 429 with a revoke route. Fixed on the way: an encoded `/media/` path logged verbatim; Fastify echoing bad URLs in error bodies; `linked.absolutePath` leaving the service in source records. Concern: what WebKit sends as fetch metadata is unverified until Task 34f. Adversarial security review dispatched.
Task 49 review (`.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/task-49-review.md`): Spec ✅; quality Needs fixes — 1 Critical, 4 Important, 6 Minor. C1: two backups of one project in the same minute both succeed and the second overwrites the first; the manifest and one `backup.create` event then record a hash the file does not have and the only backup left will not restore (probed; a double click or the scheduler is enough). I1: a governance change between the decision and the commit does not stop the backup. I2: when the fresh backup after a destruction is refused, old backups holding destroyed raw data stay for good. I3: destruction landing between the archive and the commit leaves a listed backup holding a destroyed file while the certificate says none (narrow; predates the task). I4: the governance README says backups are decided like storing, but storing never checks residency. Reviewer: failing closed on an unclassifiable location matches ADR 0005 and `planMove`; keep it and make the reason actionable. Fix round 1/5 sent to the same implementer.
- Ruling R138 (backups; completes R125 after the Task 49 review): (a) backup, prune, restore and destruction of raw data are mutually exclusive per project (a maintenance lock that does not block ordinary writes); (b) an archive never overwrites an existing file, names are unique, and the recorded hash is checked before `backup.create` is written; (c) the policy decision is evaluated again at the commit point, under the write queue, against the governance then in force; a backup that is no longer allowed is discarded and the refusal audited; (d) at the commit point a backup is also discarded if raw data was destroyed since its snapshot; (e) destruction removes every backup that holds a destroyed asset whether or not a new backup can then be made; if none can, the project has no backup, `backup-failed` is raised and the certificate says so with the reason. — why: a backup that will not restore, or one that keeps destroyed data, is worse than no backup — cost if wrong: after a destruction in a project whose location rules refuse backups the researcher has no backup until the rules or the location change; the warning and the certificate tell them.

Task 34a review (`.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/task-34a-review.md`): Spec ✅; quality Approved with fixes requested — 0 Critical, 1 Important, 9 Minor; no clause of R135 found broken; service suite 131 pass. I1: the content-security policy rule of R135 (4) has no exact test. Minors of note: an error before the first byte answers in Fastify's own shape at error level (no path or ticket leaks); the token check is skipped for any path that looks like `/media/…` rather than only the matched route (22 spellings tried, no bypass today); encoded spellings of the path are served; a stalled open connection keeps a ticket alive up to 8 hours; no tests that the hard limit or a close ends a stream in flight (a probe shows both work). What a leaked ticket allows: a local program, or a web page that sends no Referer or Origin, can stream that one recording until it is revoked, idles out or reaches 8 hours; web pages get playback they cannot read. What WebKit sends as fetch metadata is unverified until Task 34f. Fix round 1/5 sent to the same implementer.
- Ruling R140 (media tickets; completes R135): (a) another web page being able to play, not read, a recording through a leaked ticket until it is revoked or expires is accepted (the cost R135 states); (b) "idle" means no bytes sent: the 10-minute limit is refreshed only by bytes written, and a stalled connection is closed; (c) the token exemption belongs to the matched media route only, and only the exact path form is served; (d) the UI package carries a test asserting the exact content-security policy. — cost if wrong: (a) a researcher who pastes a ticket address somewhere exposes that one recording for up to 8 hours; the address is never shown as text.

Task 61 review (`.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/task-61-review.md`): Spec ✅; quality Needs fixes — 0 Critical, 3 Important, 10 Minor. I1: after a crash between the key-file write and the audit event, a read-only open does not roll back, so the old recovery code is refused and only the never-shown new code and the passphrase unlock, with no audit event (probed). I2: the wrong-passphrase limit is bypassed by parallel requests (20 concurrent wrong passphrases all got 401). I3: the new-project dialog and the library notice still call a new recovery code "planned". Reviewer's judgements: the commit point (the allow audit event) is correct under R26; `store` is a defensible policy request and consistent with other settings changes; the limit is sound per project apart from I2. Fix round 1/5 sent to the same implementer.
- Ruling R141 (new recovery code; refines R134 c and ADR 0006): the new recovery wrapping is staged beside the active one and becomes active only after the allow audit event is written; the active key file is never replaced before the commit point, so an uncommitted change is invisible to every open, read-only included. After a crash between commit and activation the next writable open completes the activation and raises a persistent warning that a new code was issued and may not have been shown; until then the old code keeps working. In no state does neither code work. Wrong-passphrase attempts are counted before verification and verified one at a time per project, across unlock and this route. — why: a rollback cannot be relied on when the library is opened read-only — cost if wrong: in the commit-then-crash case the researcher's old code stops working at the next writable open; the warning tells them and the passphrase still opens the project.
Task 51 review (`.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/task-51-review.md`): Spec ✅ (scenario 7's end-to-end check belongs to Task 44); quality Needs fixes — 0 Critical, 2 Important, 8 Minor. I-1: each job status change fetches the project key again, so locking a project while its job runs leaves the journal at "running" and re-runs the job at the next launch; a lock before "running" leaves the worker job running untracked (both probed). I-2: finished jobs are never pruned (20,000 jobs → 8.4 MB journal, 715 ms on every launch, rebuild and unlock). Reviewer's judgements: dropping the job journal on restore is right (pending proxies and OCR live in `source.json`; captures are never resumed; keeping it would run jobs against the original project); the service's stale database handle after a rebuild is real, pre-existing, low impact, a one-line fix in `state.ts`. Fix round 1/5 sent to the same implementer.
- Ruling R142 (jobs and locking; journal growth): a job that started while its project was unlocked may finish, sealing its journal lines with the key it captured at start (the key it already uses for its sealed output) and dropping it when it ends; a job still queued when its project is locked is cancelled in the worker, recorded as cancelled because of the lock, and resubmitted at the next unlock. Compaction drops succeeded jobs older than 30 days or beyond the most recent 500 per project; failed and cancelled jobs stay until retried, superseded or dismissed, capped at 500; unfinished jobs are never dropped. — why: a lock must not turn a finished job into a repeated one, and the journal must not grow without bound — cost if wrong: a job's key outlives the lock until the job ends (minutes for a long transcription); job history older than the cap is not kept.
Task 49: fix round 2/5 (N1–N5 addressed — the certificate names each backup as removed with the time or not removed with the reason; names after repeated collisions; an explicit lock hold; commit-time refusals carry the attempt's id; commits 91f78f4..fdb61e9). The implementer also added an automatic retry of pending backup removals (on open, on unlock, hourly). Re-review round 2: the retry is confined to finishing removals after a confirmed destruction and never starts or repeats one. Open, both new and Important: R2-I1 a removal that always fails is retried hourly for ever with 3 audit events each time (72 a day); R2-I2 a later destruction clears the pending warning while an earlier backup holding destroyed data is still on disk. Also asked: the certificate is amended by adding, never by replacing an earlier "not removed" entry; the retry gets tests of its own. Fix round 3/5 sent to the same implementer.

Task 37d: implemented (worktree branch `worktree-agent-a967e5533832bba87`, 6 commits, head 72cb7cc; report `.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/task-37d-report.md`): a one-folder PyInstaller build of the worker with a build script, a manifest and a smoke test; bundle 540 MB, ready in about 0.2 s; smoke 25 of 25 (health, HEIC image proxy, video proxy with a supplied ffmpeg, transcription with a model on disk, OCR paths), also with the Intel pin versions built on Linux; the library's worker-client tests pass against the packaged worker with no code change. Task 34c review (`.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/task-34c-review.md`): Spec ✅; quality Needs fixes — 0 Critical, 4 Important, 10 Minor. I1: a recording that fails mid-stream is renewed for ever (each `loadeddata` resets the guard; 7 tickets from 6 failures in the probe). I2: re-transcribe — the service has no job route and passes no progress or cancel signal; leaving and returning lets a second run start; a dropped connection says "could not be reached" while the run continues. I3: saving an edit gives every segment `flags: null` and that version opens by default, so flagged segments the person never touched show no flag (SPEC 5.8). I4: every segment change scrolls the list and the window back to the playing segment with no way to turn it off. Fix round 1/5 sent to the same implementer.
- Ruling R143 (transcripts; splits the Task 34c findings): in the UI now — the renewal guard is reset only by real playback progress, at most two renewals without it; "Follow playback" is a toggle that scrolls only the segment list and pauses while the researcher edits, focuses or scrolls; a human-edit version shows the machine flags on segments whose timing and text are unchanged and marks changed segments "edited by a person — not checked"; re-transcribe says honestly that a run may still be going when its outcome is unknown. In the service, as new Task 62 — transcription runs as a job with progress events, one run per source at a time, its state readable after a reload; a human-edit version stores flags per segment (carried over where the text is unchanged, none where the person changed it). — why: SPEC 5.8 requires that flagged text is never shown as if it were clean, and a researcher must not start the same transcription twice by leaving a screen — cost if wrong: until Task 62 the flag carry-over is computed in the UI from two versions and could mis-match re-timed segments; they are then marked as edited, which errs on the side of caution.
Task 37d review (`.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/task-37d-review.md`): Spec ✅; quality Approved with fixes — 0 Critical, 2 Important, 8 Minor. I1: the smoke test is weak on three acceptance checks (image proxies checked by status and size only; video by the worker's own metadata; transcription does not assert the words; a missing fixture or model turns a check non-required). I2: the manifest marks `av` and `pillow_heif` as not copyleft although GPL libraries are vendored inside them, and their GPL texts are not in the bundle. Licence facts confirmed by the reviewer: gallery-dl is GPL-2.0-only and runs as a second process of the same executable, not imported; libx264 and libx265 sit in `av.libs` (and libx265 in `pillow_heif.libs`) and are loaded in-process, with libavcodec reporting itself LGPL-3+; LGPL and MPL parts are in-process except ocrmypdf, which also runs as a second process; `av.libs` is what the `av` wheel needs for audio decoding (72 MB of FFmpeg libraries, with GPL encoders the worker never uses), not an ffmpeg program; no ffmpeg, tesseract, ghostscript, yt-dlp or model files are in the bundle. Model loading contacts the hub on every load (no local-only setting, no pinned revision). 64 symbolic links in `_internal` will matter for macOS signing. Fix round 1/5 sent, including a local-only model load when the model is on disk (R144).
- Ruling R144 (worker; found by Task 37d): a speech model that is already on disk is loaded with no network contact; only an absent model is downloaded, as an explicit, logged step, with a plain "not installed and could not be downloaded" error on failure. The automatic first download stays until the first-use tools dialogue (Task 48) replaces it. — why: a project that must stay offline must not need the network to transcribe, and a routine load must not announce itself to a third party — cost if wrong: a model updated upstream is not picked up until the researcher asks for it, which is the behaviour wanted.
Task 34e review (`.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/task-34e-review.md`): Spec ❌ on one point; quality Needs fixes — 0 Critical, 2 Important, 8 Minor. I1: the screen tells the researcher to type this app's port into the extension but never shows the port, so pairing cannot be completed. I2: "used" is detected against the list from the last read rather than the list at issue time, so a valid code can be hidden as "used" (probed). Fix round 1/5 sent.

- Ruling R146 (capture; splits the Task 34b findings): in the UI now — an outcome the screen did not see is shown as unknown, never as "not tried", and no summary claims more than was seen; the library's own sentence for a stopped or partial run is shown, never replaced; the UI's classifier copy is a preview hint only and never refuses an address. In the service, as new Task 63 — a route to re-read a capture queue's state (so a running capture is visible after a reload and cannot be duplicated by accident); a failed tools update mapped to a proper error; one definition of the address classifier and of the acknowledgement text, in a package both the library and the UI may import. — cost if wrong: until Task 63 a researcher who reloads during a capture cannot see it; the screen says so.
Task 49: fix round 3/5 (R2-I1 and R2-I2 addressed — the first failure and its reason are recorded at the destruction, hourly restarts add no events, the warning stands while any destruction has a backup left; measured audit events: 1 in 48 hours and 29 in 30 days when a fresh backup was made, 2 and 58 when it was refused; commits fdb61e9..7593645). Re-review round 3: NOT ready to merge. New Critical R3-C1, introduced by this round's guard: if the researcher deletes the source after a destruction whose backup removal failed, the next retry marks the backup "deleted" and clears the warning while the backup holding destroyed data stays on disk, never retried. Minors: a backup deleted by hand is recorded as "deleted" rather than as found missing; the certificate file is still replaced on amendment although its content is only added to; the locked-project skip has no effective test.
- Ruling R147 (Task 49, taken back by the controller after three fix rounds and re-scoped): which backups must be removed after a destruction is decided ONCE, at the destruction, and stored as a fixed list of backup files for that destruction; nothing later re-derives it from the project's current sources. An entry leaves the list only when its file is verifiably gone: removed by the app (recorded as removed, with the time) or found missing (recorded as no longer present, with the time it was noticed — never as removed by the app). The warning stands while any list has an entry. The certificate is amended by appending; its earlier bytes are never rewritten. The automatic retry may only delete files named on such a list. Round 4 goes to a fresh implementer on the most capable tier (R127), working in the same worktree. — why: three rounds each fixed the findings and introduced a new one in logic the task never asked for (the retry's own reasoning about what was destroyed) — cost if wrong: a backup that was removed for another reason stays listed until the app notices the file is gone, which errs on the side of the warning staying.
Task 61: fix round 1/5 (I1–I3 addressed: the new code is staged and activated only after the commit point, crashed at every seam — only the old code works before it, the new code plus a standing warning after it, the passphrase always works, both codes never work at once; 20 parallel wrong passphrases give 5 refusals and 15 "too many attempts", shared by unlock and the new-code route; wording points to "New recovery code…"; commits 6c7cb6c..ef05812). Re-review: one new Important, N1 — a staged file is tied to the audit log by its issue id only, so a stale or planted staged file that reuses a past issue id is activated: in probes this made a revoked code work again and, with damaged contents, stopped the passphrase opening the project. Minors M-a to M-d.
- Ruling R148 (Task 61, completes R141): a staged recovery-code file is activated only if ALL hold — its sha256 equals the one recorded in the audit event that allowed the issue; that event is the newest allowed issue for the project and nothing after it records an activation or a later issue; its passphrase wrapping and key id are identical to the active file's; and the audit chain verifies. Otherwise it is never activated and the active file is untouched: if the chain verifies and shows the file is not the committed one it is removed and a warning raised; if the chain does not verify it is left in place and reported. The replaced file is NOT kept aside, because keeping it would keep a revoked code usable. Also in this round: a failure after the new file is in place is reported as "activated, not shown" (M-b), and a refused issue is not labelled "A new recovery code was issued" (M-d). M-a (a read-only open in the instant between activation and clean-up shows no warning) and M-c (the attempt limiter has no time limit on one attempt) are deferred. — why: a cloud-folder client restoring a deleted file, or an older copy of the folder merged back, would otherwise silently replace the working code — cost if wrong: a genuine staged file left by a crash is refused when the audit record is damaged, and the researcher issues a code again.

A test that asserts a speed keeps its own explicit limit, and those are never loosened (the scale test, "in under 1 s" checks, ReviewQueue's 40 s). — why: on two-processor runners, and on this machine with a dozen agents at load 25–30, tests failed for slowness that no researcher would meet, and hid real results — cost if wrong: a genuinely hung test takes 20–30 s to fail instead of 5.

- Ruling R151 (owner's question, 2026-11-11: is a paid Apple developer membership needed for this build?): no. Building the Mac app in GitHub's CI and the owner's own hands-on tests (Task 55) need no paid membership: the app is signed ad hoc, and on first launch the owner allows it once in System Settings, Privacy & Security. The paid Apple Developer Program (99 US dollars a year) is needed only for Task 37g, signing with a Developer ID and notarisation, which is what lets other people open the download without a warning. Task 55 therefore depends on Task 37f (the unsigned build) and 37i, no longer on 37g; Task 37g stays blocked on the owner's credentials and is required before anything is given to anyone else. The first-run guide (Task 37i) describes the one-time "Open Anyway" step for the unsigned build. — cost if wrong: if an ad-hoc-signed build cannot be opened on the owner's Mac at all, Task 55 waits for 37g as first planned; to be confirmed with the first Mac build.
Dispatched: review of Task 37a; Task 50 (no silent refusals; its restore item waits for Task 49 to merge and is done last); Task 48a (first-use tools: worker and service half). Task 48 is split in two: 48a worker and service, 48b the dialogue in the interface.
- Ruling R152 (Task 48a; assumed until the owner decides the project's licence): gallery-dl, which is GPL-2.0-only, is no longer shipped inside the worker bundle. It becomes an optional first-use download like the video downloader, pinned by version and checksum and run as a separate program as before. This amends R126 and ADR 0009, which had it pinned inside the worker. — why: it keeps the app's own download free of a GPL-2.0-only component whatever licence the owner chooses, at the price of one more first-use download — cost if wrong: if the owner is content to ship it, it goes back into the bundle with a one-line packaging change.
Task 37a: review — spec met; build and smoke run pass; the html-strip time-outs were machine load (66 of 66 in the worktree and on the working branch, same timing). Needs fixes: I1 (Important) the built folder is not closed — a require that misses inside it goes on to parent folders, the home folder's .node_modules and NODE_PATH (a module planted at ~/.node_modules/supports-color was loaded through debug at start-up), and NAPI_RS_NATIVE_LIBRARY_PATH and NODE_OPTIONS can redirect what is loaded; the smoke guard blocked only the repository. Seven Minors. Merge consequence of Task 37b: the smoke run, START.txt and ADR 0013 must pass the token on standard input. Fix round 1 dispatched.
- Ruling R153 (Tasks 37a and 37c): the shipped service loads code only from its own folder. No run-time require may miss (the whole dependency closure of anything left outside the bundle is in the folder; optional requires are bundled or stubbed at build time), proven by a smoke run with modules planted in the home folder, a parent folder and NODE_PATH. Variables that redirect what Node loads (NODE_OPTIONS, NODE_PATH, NAPI_RS_NATIVE_LIBRARY_PATH) cannot be neutralised from inside the program, so the service refuses to start in the built layout when one is set, and the desktop shell (Task 37c) starts the service and the worker with an allow-listed environment, never its own. — why: a signed app whose helper loads a module from the home folder gives any program that can write there the unlocked projects' keys — cost if wrong: a researcher who has such a variable set globally and starts the folder by hand gets a refusal with a plain message; the shell never passes it.

Not done, by design: restoring a backup asks the policy engine (waits for Task 49). Concerns carried to review: the library-level log is shown on no screen yet and has no anchor against truncation; no acknowledge action; a mark is written into a project folder even when another device holds its lock; one test file was written after its code. Review dispatched.
Task 50: review — needs fixes: 5 Important, 8 Minor (the restore item was excluded as planned). I1 the new library-level audit log has no anchor, so cutting its end off or deleting it shows no warning; I2 refusals and late entries are recorded but shown nowhere (needs a route to read and verify the library-level log, a view of it, the audit screen showing late entries and marks, and a state update after a refused creation, relink and delete-old); I3 a planted "permissions not set" record carrying mode 0o777 made project.json world-writable and was audited as "restricted"; I4 some marks can never clear (a broken chain, an unreadable record), so the banner then says something false, and there is no way to acknowledge; I5 deleting the old copy after a move writes marks into a new copy that another device has locked.
- Ruling R154 (Task 50; completes R28 and R82): (a) the library-level audit log is protected exactly like a project's — hash-chained, anchored, written through the crash-safe path — and a cut-off or missing log raises a warning; it never grows without bound from repeated refusals of the same kind (repeats are counted on the latest entry, as the scheduler's one-denial-a-day rule does). (b) Never silent means shown: the service can read and verify the library-level log, the audit screen has a view of it, late entries and attention marks are shown where the researcher reviews the audit record, and the interface is told when one appears. (c) A repair never takes a file mode, a path or any other instruction from a record on disk: it applies the fixed restrictive mode the code defines, and audits what it actually did. (d) A mark that cannot be repaired can be set aside by the researcher: a decided, audited action with a reason, recorded in the project's log when that is intact and in the library-level log when it is not; the banner then stops asking, and the audit screen keeps the mark, the reason, who and when. Nothing is ever cleared without either a repair or that record. (e) Nothing is written under another device's lock: a mark that belongs in a project this device does not hold is kept in a device-local outbox in the app's settings folder, shown on this device, and delivered when this device next holds the lock. — why: each gap let a refusal or a failed record stay invisible, or let a file on disk steer a privileged repair — cost if wrong: one more log to verify at open and one more action on the audit screen. (The earlier open question for the owner, a "set aside" button for a project whose audit record is damaged, is the same mechanism; this ruling builds it for marks and the owner's answer decides whether it also applies to a damaged record as a whole.)
Task 50 is larger than planned (one more route, a view on the audit screen, the set-aside action, the outbox); it stays one task with its implementer, who has the context. Fix round 1 dispatched.
Task 37a: fix round 1/5 (the folder is closed: still external are better-sqlite3 and keyring (native), pdfjs-dist, exiftool-vendored, citeproc and file-type, each with its full dependency closure; the build fails on any require or import it has not accounted for; 53 stubs cover other-platform binaries and supports-color; the smoke run plants 90 module names in the home folder and a parent folder and a guard rejects any module resolved outside the folder; with NODE_PATH set the launcher refuses; the smoke run passes the token on standard input; commits 9f248d6..ae75c72; service 180 tests). Scoped re-review dispatched.
- Ruling R155 (Task 37c; amends the plan's wording for the shell): the shell starts one helper, the library service, and the service starts the media worker as it does today. Secrets travel on standard input only: the shell's token line to the service (built in Task 37b), and now the service's token line to the worker, which refuses a token in its environment or arguments. A helper exits when its standard input closes, so a parent that crashes or is force-quit leaves no stray process; the service therefore treats end-of-input after the token line as "shut down cleanly" (Task 37b had left it ignored for this purpose). Helpers get an allow-listed environment (R153). Tokens are made by the shell from the system's secure random source, new on every launch, never written to disk, a log or a command line. — why: one owner for the worker's life (restarts, job reruns) instead of two, and the same start-up path in the app, the tests and the end-to-end harness — cost if wrong: if the shell must supervise the worker directly on some platform, the worker's standard-input start-up already supports it.
Housekeeping: the working folders of merged tasks were removed (their branches remain); about 7 GB freed, 22 GB available.

Task 50: re-review of fix round 1 — needs fixes. I1 addressed (edits, cuts, deletion and a missing anchor are all detected; an anchor rolled back together with a matching cut is the same accepted limit as for projects); I3 addressed; I2, I4, I5 partly. New Important: N1 the service passes its read-only repository and marks are still written under another device's lock; N2 a subfolder or a sync client's conflict copy in the attention folder shows "Set aside…" but can never be set aside; N3 a set-aside recorded in the project's log shows no reason or mark on the audit screen; N4 an outbox entry moved into the project and followed by a crash is recorded twice. Minors N5–N10. Fix round 2 dispatched.
- Ruling R157 (Task 50; amends R154 a and d): (1) setting a mark aside is its own policy operation, allowed in every profile, and the policy engine itself refuses it without a reason, so the decision is real and its refusal audited; the owner may tighten who may do it under a profile later. (2) Repeats of the same refusal on the same day are not counted: the day's first entry of a kind is an ordinary chained entry, later identical ones that day are dropped, and the screen says so; "the same kind" means every field the entry shows is equal. No count is kept outside the chain. (3) Anything in the attention folder that is not a well-formed mark is shown once as an unrecognised item and can be set aside by moving it to a set-aside area inside the project, never deleted and never parsed. (4) Delivery from the outbox is idempotent by entry id. — why: a count outside the chain could be edited unseen, and an item that can be shown but not acted on is a warning that never clears — cost if wrong: a burst of identical refusals in one day appears as one line.

- Ruling R159 (Task 37c): (a) one settings location — when the shell starts the service it tells it to keep everything (app settings, pairings, the outbox, the tools folder) under the shell's own app data folder; without the shell the service's default is unchanged. (b) The About screen is reachable from inside an open library as well as from the chooser. (c) The About screen also lists the licences of the shell's Rust crates (MPL-2.0 ones named with where their source is) and of the npm packages bundled into the interface, generated at build time; the build fails if one has no licence entry. — why: notices must travel with what is distributed, and settings split across two folders would be lost or doubled on a move — cost if wrong: the identifier is a one-line change until the first release, and a real cost after it.

Task 58: implemented (worktree branch worktree-agent-a305bc948368414df, head 28e8368; annotation text is indexed and results say "Found in an annotation" (index schema 2, rebuilt from the files on the next open of an existing library); the time-zone field already existed and retention used it — added a governed, audited setter, PUT /projects/:id/time-zone, and a field beside the retention date; library 1171 tests, service 205; scale test 94 s; a 10,000-annotation source imports in 2.6 s). Review dispatched.
- Ruling R160 (Task 58; refines R17): when a project has a time zone set, it is the zone for every date rule of that project — protocol validity (R16) as well as retention (R93). When none is set, the computer's zone is used, as R17 says. — why: a researcher who travels would otherwise see a protocol begin or expire on a different day than the institution that approved it — cost if wrong: one more setting to explain; it is optional.
Task 37c verification after merge, remaining suites: worker 171 passed, script tests 23, desktop tests 10 — all passed.
Dispatched: Task 37h (one end-to-end scenario through the real Linux app window; the desktop build and checks as a separate CI job).
Task 58: review — spec met for what was briefed; needs fixes: 4 Important, 9 Minor. I1 after the index's schema migration the first write records fresh stamps, so the index counts as up to date with old annotations unindexed, and the normal open never rebuilds; I2 a CJK annotation set at the R74 cap costs about 1.7 GB of memory and 7.5 s of blocking per write, against the service's 512 MB ceiling; I3 annotation-only matches outrank title matches; I4 R160 is not met — ten places supply the computer's zone to the policy engine, and the bulk import's dry run evaluates in UTC while its writes use the computer's zone. (The encryption-and-index ruling is R30, not R24 as the dispatch said.) Fix round 1 dispatched.
- Ruling R160, amended (Task 58): when a project has a time zone set, it governs every date rule of that project. When none is set, each rule keeps the default it has today: the computer's zone for protocol dates (R17) and UTC for retention (R92). One function decides the zone and every caller uses it, so a dry run and its real run always agree. — why: changing retention's default to the computer's zone would make a destruction date move with the device — cost if wrong: two defaults to explain beside the control; the control says which applies.
- Ruling R161 (Task 58, the index): (a) a schema migration that leaves old content unindexed sets a durable "rebuild needed" marker that only a completed rebuild clears; while it is set the index reports stale and search answers say the index is incomplete. The rebuild itself is not added to the open-a-library path by this task: the paused Task 46a (D8) makes open rebuild a missing or stale index and will honour the marker through the staleness check; until it merges, the rebuild route and reconnect clear it. (b) At most 2 MB of annotation text is indexed per source, in document order, in bounded chunks; the remainder is not indexed and the source is marked as only partly searchable; memory and time per write are asserted in tests at the cap. — why: an index that silently lacks content is worse than one that says it is incomplete, and R64's ceiling is a hard limit — cost if wrong: a source with more than 2 MB of annotations is found by its first 2 MB only, and says so.
Task 58: fix round 1/5 (a "rebuild needed" marker that only a completed rebuild clears; annotations in their own table, 2 MB per source, indexed in chunks — a write at the cap went from 10.3 s and 1.5 GB to about 0.3 s and 23 MB; column weights; one function for a project's zone, so a dry run and its run agree; M1–M9; commits b920846..9679c86; library 1221 tests, core 437, service 211, UI 844; scale test 93.7 s alone). Re-review: I1–I4 addressed with the probes re-run (R30 holds; incremental equals rebuilt). One Important left, N1: a query matches only if the record, or one single 64 KB chunk of annotation text, holds every term — an author's name plus a word from an annotation finds nothing, and two annotations of one source match or not depending on a chunk boundary. Not acceptable under R161: chunking must never decide what is found. Minors N2 (the ranking test passes without the weights), N3 (the "incomplete" note says a rebuild is running when none is), N4 (the asserted memory bound omits the index's own blocks). Fix round 2 dispatched.
GitHub: run 178 (51a4d31, Task 48a) success. Run 179 (f31885d, Task 48b) ended "cancelled" after about 70 minutes, and run 180 (03f5801, Task 37c) failed. Two causes, both the controller's misses at merge, found by the Task 37h implementer: (1) the first-use "Optional tools" question added by Task 48b is a modal dialog over the whole app, and the browser end-to-end harness did not answer it, so every app scenario waited on it until the job's time limit; (2) the root `pnpm test` runs Vitest over every workspace package and picked up the desktop package's two node:test files added by Task 37c ("No test suite found"). Neither was caught because the merges were verified package by package and without the app end-to-end suite.
Fixed by the controller (R127): e2e/fixtures.ts records the first-use question as answered "Not now", the way the app records it, before any app code runs (an answer already there is left alone, so a scenario about the question can set its own state); apps/desktop/vitest.config.ts keeps Vitest away from the node:test files and the Rust folder. The app end-to-end suite was running locally to confirm when this was pushed; its result is in the next entry.
- Ruling R162 (controller's merge checks): before a merge is pushed, the controller runs the root `pnpm test` (not only the packages a task names) and, for any task that changes the interface, the service, the worker or the harness, the app end-to-end suite. — why: two merges in a row broke the build in ways that only the whole-repository commands show — cost if wrong: about fifteen more minutes a merge on this machine.
Defect D9 (from the Task 37h report, seen in the real window): the first-use question opens asynchronously over the library chooser and can take focus from a person who is already typing. For a follow-up to Task 48b: ask only once a library is open and the app is idle, never while a text field has focus.

New: N5 (Important, speed) at 5,000 annotated sources two common words take 1.1–1.2 s and six take 3.4 s on the service's one thread; N6 (Minor) the 50,000 cap counts matches across the whole library, so a project's search can be refused because of other projects. Fix round 3 dispatched: look-ups scoped to the searched project inside the query, combining in the database, an asserted time budget.

Task 48c: review — needs fixes: 2 Important, 5 Minor. I1 the question can still open in the middle of a file drag or while the window is in the background, and the first drop is then swallowed; scrolling, a screen reader reading and focus inside a frame do not hold it back; the timing tests use real waits. I2 R163 not met (the implementer had not been given it). The new browser scenario passes in both browsers. Fix round 1 dispatched, with the design simplified by R164.
- Ruling R164 (Task 48c; refines R126 and R163): the first-use question never opens by itself. Until it is answered, the open library's screen shows a non-modal banner that asks it in one sentence, with "Choose tools…" (which opens the dialogue, by the researcher's own action) and "Not now" (which records the answer). It never takes focus, is announced once, and appears on no other screen. Reaching the Optional tools screen by any route counts as an answer. The idle-detection rule and its timers are removed. — why: a dialog that opens by itself changes context without the person asking, and each condition added to make that safe (focus, drags, a background window, a screen reader reading) left another gap — cost if wrong: the owner asked for "a dialogue on first use"; the dialogue is now one click from a banner that asks, rather than appearing unprompted. The owner can ask for the unprompted form back.

The main job failed in the scale test alone: 122.3 s against its 120 s budget, memory 336 MB. Not a regression: the same test on the same library code took 63.6 s in run 182; earlier runs 85.3 s (173) and 97.1 s (177). Shared two-processor runners vary by a factor of two.
- Ruling R166 (CI, the scale test): the test's own budget (10,000 items in under 120 s with under 500 MB) is unchanged. In CI a failed attempt is repeated once, and every attempt's time is written to the run summary so a real slowdown shows as a trend. — why: consecutive runs of identical code measured 64 s and 122 s on GitHub's runners, so a single attempt fails by chance — cost if wrong: a slowdown that leaves the time between about 100 and 120 s on a fast runner can pass for a while; the summary shows it.

A package whose own files give no licence text gets the standard text for its declared identifier and is listed as such; a NOTICE file is not a licence text. What a package bundles without shipping texts for it (the canvas binary's components) is listed by name as a known gap, never filled with invented text. — why: a licence text edited by a tool is no longer the licence — cost if wrong: a longer notices file.

2026-11-12 08:30 UTC — THE FIRST MAC RUN (GitHub run 43179251870, "Desktop (macOS)" run 1, Apple Silicon on macos-15, macOS 15.8.2, commit 8a5417a): the app was built and every check passed except the last. 15 minutes from start to end without any cache (the estimate was 55–75; billed at about ten times, so about 150 minutes).
What ran on a Mac for the first time and worked: tauri-cli installed (3 min); the pinned Node archive; the worker packaged by PyInstaller with its manifest (56 packages, 53 native libraries); the Tauri bundler accepted the Mac configuration and made the release app (461 MiB) and the debug app; ad-hoc signing (209 Mach-O files, 207 already signed, 3 codesign calls; `codesign --verify --deep --strict` valid on the built app and again on the app in the mounted disk image); the disk image made by hdiutil, verified and mounted (the download: 205 MB as uploaded); the packaged worker's smoke test, 28 of 28 (health, token refusal, HEIC, proxies, transcription with the tiny model, imports); the launch check A–D on the debug app — the window is visible; the page reached the service and the worker from the origin `tauri://localhost` (requests, the event stream, a blob picture, audio and video by ticket); the helpers got an allow-listed environment (read through `ps -E`, which works there) and no token outside standard input; the 512 MB ceiling; a second launch hands over; a normal close, SIGTERM and SIGKILL each leave no helper; a service that refuses to start is reported in its own words and the shell exits by itself.
So the risks carried from the reviews are settled for Apple Silicon: the web view does NOT block the page's calls to http://127.0.0.1 (the `localhost` fallback of ADR 0013 and R167 c is not needed); ad-hoc signing of the programs and libraries under Contents/Resources works; the bundler accepts the configuration; hdiutil works; `ps -E` can be read.
What failed: launch check R (the release app run from the mounted disk image) timed out after 180 s "waiting for the service and the worker under the shell". Cause, found by the controller in the code: the check's own expectation was wrong. The service starts the worker when a library is opened (apps/library-service/src/state.ts), and nothing opens one in scenario R — the release app has no probe page and the check holds no token. The shell did not exit during the wait and no process was left afterwards, which fits a running service and no worker; the run's output cannot prove the service had started, because the check reported only the combined condition. Fixed in the check (reviewed by the controller, R127): R now waits for the service alone (120 s), prints the processes under the shell and the shell's output if it does not appear, checks a worker only if one is running, and says in a note that none is expected. The worker from an app's own resources stays proved by A–D on the debug app, which is staged and signed by the same steps.
Build request 2 (Apple Silicon) is made by the commit that carries this entry.
Still unverified after any Mac run, and left to the owner's hands-on tests (Task 55): opening the downloaded app from Finder with the quarantine mark a browser download carries ("Open Anyway", R151); scenario E (opened again while closing), skipped on a Mac; the release app's page as a person sees it.

Important: I1 some labels quoted in six checks exist only for screen readers, so the owner could never see them; I2 a check expects "no sources" where an earlier scenario's article is still there; I3 a check expects right-to-left titles at the right of their cell, which D7 prevents; I4 opening Optional tools in scenario 4 dismisses the first-use banner for good, so scenario 9's banner checks would fail; I5 a product defect, below. - Ruling R171 (defect D13, found by this review; Task 66): the library service takes a new port at every launch (the shell asks for any free port) and the extension keeps the port typed at pairing and never looks for another (R110: it never scans), so every paired browser stops working each time the app is reopened. Decided: the service listens again on the port it used last time when that port is free, and falls back to another free port when it is not; when the port changed, the pairing screen says so and the popup lets the researcher enter the new port without pairing again, the token staying valid; the extension still never scans; 127.0.0.1 only, token, Host and Origin checks unchanged. Until Task 66 lands, the guide and checklist say plainly that the browser must be paired again after each relaunch ("Known problem D13"). — why: a pairing that dies at every launch makes browser capture unusable in practice, and no test met it because every test pairs and captures within one run of the service — cost if wrong: a stable port is slightly easier for another local program to find; it still needs the token, and the Host and Origin checks stand.

2026-11-12 10:12 UTC — Task 49 round 4 reviewed (a fresh reviewer, with probes, against R147): for DELETION the ruling holds — no edit of a certificate, an amendment or the state file made the app delete anything it should keep; the event counts hold (1 and 29; 2 and 58); the locked-project test pins its guard; crash cases settle once. For what is WRITTEN it does not yet: 2 Important, 4 Minor. I1: a restore rewrites the ids inside the copied certificates, so a restored project reports every inherited certificate as "changed afterwards", for good, and writes that false claim into its audit log; it never removes a stuck backup it inherited, and a reused name keeps its warning for good (the implementer's report had this case wrong, unprobed). I2: when the chain does not verify, an observe-only pass still writes "no longer present" with an empty list, against R174 (a).
- Ruling R175 (Task 49, round 5): (a) while a project's audit chain does not verify, a retry pass deletes nothing, ends no warning and writes nothing that says or implies completion, on every path. (b) A destruction certificate and its amendments are evidence: a restore copies them byte for byte and never rewrites ids inside them; readers map ids when reading. The duty to see listed backups removed follows the data: a restored project keeps the inherited outstanding list and settles it as the original would, and the two never record the same step twice. (c) Accepted as a limit of the audit log itself, not of this task: an event correctly chained and appended while the app is closed is believed at the next open — the reviewer forged a destruction event that way and the next retry removed a backup holding only live data. Whoever can append to the log can delete the file directly; the anchor detects rewriting and truncation, not appending. It goes to the final review (Task 54) as a question about the audit log's design (a keyed chain, or an anchor outside the folder), not into this task. — why (c): a fifth round on this task must not grow a new defence that would itself need rounds; the limit is general and is stated where the module's limits are described — cost if wrong: an attacker with write access to the closed library can make the app, rather than themselves, delete a backup, and the audit log will show the app did it.

- Ruling R176 (how verification entries are resolved; completes R172): (1) an entry queued by a capture with conflicting fields is resolved by the library, in the same governed, audited, crash-safe change as the confirm, once none of the fields it lists is left in conflict or unconfirmed; a recheck that finds them no longer in conflict resolves it too; there is no separate "dismiss", because keeping the present value is confirming it. (2) An entry queued by a consent withdrawal is resolved only by the researcher's explicit decision, "Reviewed after the withdrawal": its own policy operation, allowed in every profile (as setting a mark aside is, R157), with a required reason in the researcher's words, audited; it changes nothing else — removing or destroying the source is done with the tools that exist, and the entry says so. (3) New entries record what queued them; the two kinds are no longer told apart by whether fields are listed. (4) Removing a source resolves its pending entries in the same change. — why: a queue entry nobody can clear is a permanent false alarm, and a consent withdrawal is exactly the case where the record must show that a person looked and why the source was kept or what was done — cost if wrong: "Reviewed" could be used to wave a withdrawal through; the reason is mandatory and audited, and the consent rules that restrict a withdrawn participant's sources are untouched by it.
- For the owner, as a decision they may want to change: what "reviewing" a source after a consent withdrawal should require. The ruling asks for a reason and nothing more; stricter choices exist (a second confirmation, a fixed list of grounds for keeping the source).

Both start a folder import of 20 small PDFs, wait for 2 to be done, replace the batch's suspend with one that never settles, and expect a close or a move to give up after 300 ms and cancel or refuse. They assume the import is still running then. On GitHub's quiet machine the other 18 PDFs are imported within those 300 ms and the batch finishes by itself; on this loaded machine it never does, which is why every local verification passed. Nothing so far says the product is wrong: a batch that finishes by itself before a close gives up on it is legitimately finished.

One implementer (new worktree) is making the two tests hold their batch at a gate the test controls, not by size or time, with the instruction to stop and report if it finds the product at fault, and to list other tests with the same assumption. (b) In an encrypted project the reason given for "Reviewed after the withdrawal" is not written to the audit log in plain text: the audit entry records that a reason was given and its SHA-256, and the text is kept only in the sealed review entry; in a project that is not encrypted it is audited as typed. (c) An import's undo resolves a removed source's entries in the same change that removes it. — why (b): an encrypted project's audit log can be read without its key, and a withdrawal reason is the most likely place for a participant's name — cost if wrong: the reason of an encrypted project cannot be read from the audit screen while the project is locked. (b) `device.json` never gets a new id over a file that merely could not be read (the id says which device holds a library's lock; a changed id makes this device see its own lock as another's): a read error refuses; damaged content is kept aside, a new id is made, and an attention mark says the device's identity was renewed and what that means for a library it left locked. — why: both files had the fault D16 names, but a reset would forget which old copies await deletion, and a silent new device id locks a researcher out of their own library — cost if wrong: a passing read error stops a library move or an open with a plain message instead of going on.

Ruling R192 (the reader; the plan's six questions): (a) annotation changes reuse the policy actions `store` and `delete`, with their own audit operation names; (b) the content-security policy gains `'self'` in `connect-src` and nothing else, identically in both places, with a test that keeps them identical; (c) recolouring an imported highlight counts as the researcher's edit, and a deleted imported highlight is remembered so that a re-import never brings it back; (d) the largest PDF the reader opens is 512 MiB for now (not the plan's 1 GiB: pdf.js allocates the file's whole length), revisited after the owner has tried a large scan on the Mac; (e) a note is saved by "Save note", with a question before a typed note is abandoned; (f) a search hit opens the reader by matching its words among the document's annotations, with no index migration. 2026-11-13 00:15 UTC — **Task 72, review: Needs fixes — 0 Critical, 2 Important, 4 Minor.** Sound under probe: crash safety at every crash point, two checks at once, a check racing a confirmation, settling, removal, the undo. I1: accepting an import candidate for a field that the item's own import entry lists puts the field in conflict and leaves no pending entry, because the still-pending import entry was counted as "already listing" it (the usual real case; the test's import entry listed no fields). I2: for the same reason a later check on a source whose pending import entry lists the field queues nothing, and once the item is accepted the conflict has no entry.
Ruling R193 (amends R190): (a) only a pending entry of the capture-conflict kind counts as already listing a field; (b) the rule is an invariant: after any governed change that writes a source's verification report (a capture, a later check, an import decision), every field in conflict that the researcher has not confirmed is listed in a pending capture-conflict entry of that source, written or extended in the same change — whether the conflict is new or was already there without an entry, so a source hit by D19 before the fix is put right at its next check, with no separate repair pass; an ordinary edit still queues nothing (R177); (c) the rest of R190 stands. — cost if wrong on (b): a "Needs review" appearing on an old source at its next check, which is the truth about it.
Fix round 1 sent to the same implementer. The joint verification of 71–73, started on the earlier head, was stopped and will be run again on the fixed one.

I8: from pdf.js's source, 2^27 canvas bytes downscale 600 dpi A4 and Letter scans about 2.3 times, against the report's claim, and 2^26 pixels drop a 600 dpi A3 or a 1200 dpi bitonal page, leaving a blank page with no message.
Ruling R201 (the reader; written into the plan as Amendment A7): (a) pdf.js is never handed made-up bytes: when a range is refused or fails after opening, `failed` settles, the pdf.js document and its worker are destroyed at once so that every call in flight rejects, and the handle is dead; to go on, the reader opens the document again; (b) a worker that cannot be constructed is a `PdfOpenError` like any other failure to start; (c) the limits are 2^28 pixels and 2^28 canvas bytes; a dropped picture is told on the page only if pdf.js offers a supported signal, otherwise it is a recorded limit (a page whose only picture is larger than 268 megapixels is drawn blank); (d) binding on Task R7: every read from a handle is raced against `failed`; on failure every render is cancelled, every canvas and text layer cleared, every late result dropped, nothing from that handle ever stored, the handle never reused; (e) Tasks R8, R10 and R11 make a highlight only from a live handle, checked again at the moment of saving. Fix round 2 sent to the same implementer; the same reviewer will probe it again.
**This is the second time a "small" mechanism the implementer itself flagged turned out to be the serious fault** (Task 62's mixed transcript version was the first): a note "needs your look" in a report is sent to the reviewer as a question, never accepted on reading.
