The last run that worked was 211 (03:19–03:50, green). This is the symptom of a private repository's included Actions minutes being used up or its spending limit reached; the controller cannot see the billing page and has told the owner where to look (Settings, Billing and plans, usage for Actions). Only the owner can put it right.
**The controller's own slip, the same hour:** the documents merge of the working branch into the reader branch at 04:27 also carried `apps/desktop/mac-build-request.json` (request 4), which is the trigger of the Mac workflow on any `claude/**` branch — so it asked for a Mac build of the reader branch, unintended. It failed at once like everything else and cost nothing; with minutes available it would have cost about 120. Rule from now on: before merging the working branch into the reader branch, check whether that file differs (`git diff --quiet claude/pdf-reader-highlights claude/amanuensis-handoff-0x6u5m -- apps/desktop/mac-build-request.json`); if it does, the merge is a deliberate request for a Mac build of the reader branch or it is not made. (The two files are equal now.)
Ruling R202 (while GitHub does not start runs; told to the owner, who may reverse it): (a) reader-branch merges go ahead on the controller's verification alone — typecheck, the whole root suite, the lints, the interface build, the app end-to-end run in both browsers, and the desktop steps wherever the interface's dependencies, the policy, the notices or the shell are touched — and each is recorded as "GitHub's run not made"; (b) any test that is skipped when run as root is run as another user on this machine before its task is merged; (c) when GitHub starts runs again, its result is read on the reader branch's head before any further merge, and anything red is put right first; (d) nothing is merged to the working branch that would need a Mac build, and no Mac build is requested. — why: approved work must not sit unpushed on a machine that restarted twice tonight; the reader branch is a feature branch that nothing is released from — cost if wrong: a fault that only GitHub's cleaner machine shows (as the root-skipped test did) is found later, on a branch with more merges on it.

2026-11-13 06:08 UTC — **The plan for Zotero account sync is drafted and accepted**: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-13-zotero-account-sync.md` on the reader branch (f19165e), twelve tasks Z1–Z12. What the planner found in the code: the existing Web import cannot run unattended as it is — every run leaves a batch behind even when nothing changed; files are downloaded before the journal is checked, so a resumed run or a changed tag downloads a PDF again; a resumed run never records the library version it reached; items moved to Zotero's bin are invisible to it and deletions are only counted; a key without file access fails the whole run; a field changed on both sides is "kept and reported" with nobody asked; nothing checks what a key may read; and no stand-in for Zotero's servers exists for end-to-end tests. The plan's order keeps the scheduler (Z10) after the screen (Z9), so that no run starts by itself before the screen that shows and stops it exists.
Ruling R206 (the plan's eight questions): (1) a field changed in Zotero and here becomes a review entry the researcher decides, through the mechanism of R190/R193/R194, and a later, different value from Zotero asks again; (2) an item deleted or binned in Zotero keeps the existing "unavailable" status with a new optional field saying which, not a new status value; (3) file download hosts: `zoterofilestorage.s3.amazonaws.com`, path-style `s3.amazonaws.com` only under `/zoterofilestorage/`, and — against the planner's advice to drop it — `files.zotero.net` stays allowed (Zotero's own host; wrongly dropping it would cost the researcher their files, keeping it costs nothing); a refused host is named in the run's record; R79 stays open until the owner's real account shows the host; (4) undoing a sync batch resets that library's remembered version and pauses its automatic sync, with a sentence saying so; (5) a key that may write is accepted, with advice that reading is enough (nothing but GET is ever sent, asserted by a test); (6) settings and history are kept per computer, the remembered version in the project; (7) R197 is closed as it is; (8) collections are added on update, a rename in Zotero arrives as a new collection and the old one is kept and reported. Amendment A1 in the plan carries (3) and the rule that every run-changing task is reviewed with the reader's annotation rules in hand. **Questions for the owner (none blocks the first tasks):** whether their PDFs are in Zotero's own storage or on WebDAV or a university drive; which group libraries, into which project; whether their key may write; how often to check (30 minutes, 1 hour — the default — or 4 hours); whether an item deleted in Zotero should be marked on the source (the plan's choice) or only listed in the sync history. Working files: `impl-common-zotero.md`, `review-common-zotero.md`.

What the planner found: the existing engine hashes and reads every file it sees, so in a cloud folder its first scan would download a whole shared drive; the service's route accepts any folder path the page sends; a source the researcher deleted comes back at the next scan; a changed file becomes a new source by itself; nothing records which file became which source; Node cannot read macOS's "not downloaded" flag, so the plan asks the system's own `/usr/bin/stat`; the app's `Info.plist` lacks the sentence macOS shows when an app asks for a cloud-drive folder, without which macOS may refuse without asking (an unverified report).
Ruling R207 (the plan's six questions, all as recommended): (1) a folder is approved only through the shell's own folder chooser, which hands the path to the service as a single-use grant on the shell's existing standard-input pipe; the page never sends a path; **R98 and R155 ("anything after the token is discarded, never interpreted") are changed for exactly that one line shape and nothing else**; (2) the register of which file became which source lives in the library, sealed in encrypted projects (relative names only; noted for the pseudonymisation review, R64); (3) the plan's rule — a changed file is never imported by itself, a deleted source stays deleted — replaces R81's "change events re-import changed content"; (4) `/usr/bin/stat` is accepted as the exact check, started once per 200 suspect files, an unknown answer treated as not downloaded; (5) "Download and import them" stops after 1 GB beyond what the researcher confirmed and asks again; (6) cloud-drive folders are refused on Windows until a native check exists. Amendment A1 in the plan: W1 and W5 go to one implementer; in W2–W4 any path that reads a file before the check is a Critical finding. **Questions for the owner (none blocks the first tasks):** which of Google Drive, OneDrive and Dropbox they use, with which kind of account; whether the folders are their own or shared with colleagues; roughly how many files, and whether the folders inside should be watched too; whether Google Drive is set to "Stream files" or "Mirror files"; whether colleagues' files go straight into the project or first into the review queue; whether a note on the source is enough when a colleague changes or deletes a file. Working files: `impl-common-watch.md`, `review-common-watch.md`.

2026-11-13 06:31 UTC — **Review of cloud-folders Tasks W1 and W5: spec met for both; W5 passes as it is; W1 needs fixes** (Critical 1, Important 2, Minor 7). **C1 (in the plan's own rule, built faithfully):** on a Mac a file was called "local" from its size and block count alone, without asking the "not downloaded" flag — but macOS's block count includes other parts of a file and is made up from the size where a file system keeps none (the reviewer cites the kernel's source), and a placeholder of size 0 may exist; so a file that is not downloaded could have been judged local and then read, which downloads it. I1: a clear flag on a file short of blocks was taken as "local", true only for Apple's own placeholder format. I2: `lstat` had no time limit and ran 32 at once on Node's four file threads, so a stalled cloud service could stall the whole service. Confirmed by the reviewer from Apple's sources: `stat -f "%i %Xf"` uses `lstat`, prints all of the flags in hexadecimal without a prefix, and writes its errors apart from its output; names cannot forge an output line; W5's key name and sentence are exact.
Ruling R208 (Amendment A3 of the cloud-folders plan): (a) C1 is Critical and W1's Accept 1 is replaced for macOS — "local" rests only on the flags the system tool printed for that file's inode; every regular file is asked, size 0 included; the block count may only make an answer worse; (b) I1 — a file short of blocks with the flag clear is "local" only if it carries the compressed flag, otherwise "unknown"; (c) W1 also returns what it judged (inode, size, blocks, time), and W2 compares these immediately before opening a file; (d) I2 — in W1 at most two `lstat` calls at a time, one per path, and an abort signal after which nothing further is started; and for the engine (W2): at most one file-system call in flight per watched folder, a folder whose call has not answered in the time limit is "Cannot be reached" and gets no further call until the stuck one settles, the watcher library does no polling of its own, and the service starts with sixteen file threads; what remains is on the owner's Mac list, and if that check shows the service stalling, folder watching moves into a process of its own (a later task); (e) the check runs for every watched folder whatever the cloud-service detection says, and a folder that is or contains the whole cloud-storage area is refused in plain words; (f) a file whose state cannot be told is described as such, not as "online only"; (g) the register's keys keep letter case, and a name differing only by case or normalisation with the same content is a rename. Fix round 1 sent to W1's implementer.

2026-11-13 08:07 UTC — **Review of Zotero Task Z2: spec not met in two places; needs fixes** (Critical 1, Important 4, Minor 9). Sound: nothing but GET is sent; the host rule holds against every trick tried; the key appears nowhere; the edits outside the file list are right and harmless; the skip hook fetches nothing for a skipped item. **C1: a resumed batch recorded the library version its resumed part read at, while the engine's journal skipped the items its first part had brought in — a change made in Zotero to one of those items between the stop and the resume was never fetched, and a binned one never yielded as gone.** I1: a back-off that was waited out still held the version back (against R210). I4: the deletion note said "marked" and counted child keys and items never imported here.
Ruling R212 (Amendment A5 of the Zotero plan): (a) a resumed batch records the LOWEST library version any of its parts read at — each part's read version is kept in the batch record; if one is unknown nothing is recorded and the next run repeats; every way a batch is resumed finishes through the same function; (b) a complete scan is one whole read at one version, every paged listing carrying its total and adding up to it, no page failed or cut, the key still allowed at the end; a restart that then read everything counts; (c) **I3 is ruled Critical: absence alone never makes an item gone** — on a full run an item is yielded as gone by absence only when the scan was complete and its key is also absent from the library's complete key list, fetched in one listing at the same library version; if that listing fails or differs, nobody is gone by absence in that run; (d) the deletion note counts top-level items known here and says "nothing was deleted here"; (e) a file whose checksum never matched has its own honest sentence. Fix round 1 sent to Z2's implementer; then a scoped re-review by the same reviewer (`a3f36ba6ea5d98603`).
2026-11-13 08:19 UTC — **Review of cloud-folders Task W2: needs fixes** (Critical 1 by this task's own rule, Important 2, Minor 9). Sound: for an ordinary file the order holds on every path (an event, the first scan, the rescan, the re-check, the folder's return, an unlock, a retry); the scale test's 200 opens are real; nothing in the watched folder changes; no name or path in an audit entry. **C1: sidecars** (the citation files beside a document) were read after only a "local" answer, with no stability reading and no comparison (a `.ris` changing every 50 ms was read; exposure at most 1 MB a sidecar). I1: with one watcher per folder, a subfolder deleted and made again under the same name was neither watched nor listed until the five-minute rescan, and its old watcher was never closed. I2: the close of a handle whose read was stuck ran outside the one-call-per-folder limit. Also: the main file's later opens (the copy can come tens of seconds after the one comparison); the re-check of not-downloaded files cost five processes a second for as long as a folder held such files; `fs.watch` makes synchronous calls on the main thread when it is set up, once per folder.
Ruling R213 (Amendment A6 of the cloud-folders plan): (a) accepted — Node's `fs.watch` in place of chokidar (which holds a descriptor on every file on macOS and cost 80 MB for 40,000 files), and chokidar is removed from the package; (b) C1 stands as Critical: a sidecar is read only after the same order as any file, by itself; (c) every separate open of the watched file is guarded by a fresh comparison with the judged state — the hash, the type sniff, metadata extraction, the copy — and links are not followed; (d) on macOS a recursive watch is ONE watcher for the whole tree, set up only right after the folder answered, so that set-up makes one synchronous call; on Linux one per folder with I1 fixed; (e) every call is inside the per-folder limit, and a folder with a stuck call stays "Cannot be reached" across restarts of its watch; (f) the re-check of not-downloaded files runs at once on an event and otherwise once a minute, not every two seconds; (g) a file the system refuses to open is "not permitted", counted. On the owner's Mac list, the decisive checks for this design: quitting or pausing the cloud service's app in mid-scan (does the app stay responsive; does watching resume); that a file which is not downloaded is never opened. Fix round 1 sent to W2's implementer; then a scoped re-review by the same reviewer (`ab0130341fcb53814`).

2026-11-13 16:57 UTC — **Find and acquire: the implementation plan is written and committed on the reader branch** (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-13-find-and-acquire.md`, 8208248; 20 tasks in the design's five slices: 1 = F1–F7 the box, the queue, title-and-author lookup with choosing, the record, "Already in your library"; 2 = F8–F9 an author alone; 3 = F10–F14 free copies, the file check, "Search sources" with Elicit; 4 = F15–F19 "Your library" and the sign-in window; 5 = F20 the extension fallback). What the planner found against the design: the capture queue is memory only (the model for the queue is the watch register); provenance is per source and cannot change, so each file needs its own record of how it was got; the web view tells the shell addresses only, never types, and its cookie lookup by address misses a proxy's domain cookies; OpenAlex without a key now allows about 100 searches a day; Elicit has one search address (v2); the usual Rust HTTP client's default encryption library has a licence that does not fit AGPL.
Ruling R225 (Amendment A1 of the find-and-acquire plan): the plan's nine rulings are settled as recommended — (1) **the service's standard-input pipe carries one more line shape, `file-grant`** (a PDF the shell fetched in the sign-in window and wrote into its own inbox folder; R98/R155 as changed by R207 are changed for exactly this shape and nothing else; the shell empties the inbox at start and quit); (2) the three new ways of acquiring (`reference_lookup`, `free_copy`, `library_access`) are a list of their own, `acquireMethods`, allowed by default in every project, existing ones included (the owner's decision), because a project that ever named its capture methods would otherwise refuse them; Elicit stays a lookup service a project's rules must name; (3) the queue is `<Project>/find/queue.jsonl`, governed and sealed like the watch register; (4) the service fetches files only from a fixed list of hosts, every other free copy is a link — **and, the coordinator's addition, such a link can be opened in the app's own window and its PDF added on the researcher's press** (slice 4); (5) an optional OpenAlex key, Crossref alone when the day's allowance is spent; (6) each file records how it was got (`Asset.acquired`); (7) one new Rust dependency, `reqwest` 0.13.5 with the system's own encryption (MIT or Apache-2.0); (8) the shell knows a PDF by its address or the researcher's press and never fetches a sign-in address; its fetch sits behind one function so that the web view's own download can replace it; (9) a file that does not match is held in the project for the review queue, at most 20 per project. Common brief for implementers written (`.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/impl-common-acquire.md`). For the owner, not blocking: a free OpenAlex key; how their library signs them in. Final review's list: the queue's typed lines in an unencrypted project (R64); the format additions an older build cannot read.

(b) What recorded answers cannot say — a slow or held answer, a 429 with its headers, a file download, a redirect — is needed first by slice 3's end-to-end task (F14, free copies): THAT task decides, with the coordinator, between widening the recorded answers and a stand-in server; nothing is decided now. 2026-11-13 23:39 UTC — **Zotero Task Z8 REVIEWED on the whole** (fresh Opus reviewer `a7531734e5055e313`, 18 probes; review `.superpowers/sdd/PLAN/task-Z8-review.md`): spec not met, 0 critical, 4 important, 8 minor. Sound: the key in no response, event, log line or file; who may call; every reason has its sentence; the times; close, switch, move and shutdown; undo recognised from the batch's own record. Not sound: I1 a sync into another project starts while a manual import of the same Zotero library runs; I2 an import and a sync sent together both run (30 items became 60 sources, three times of three; two manual imports sent together do the same — an older gap); I3 the history shows the run in progress as "Stopped … the app was closed … Nothing here was changed."; I4 for a locked encrypted project a "finished with problems" line names a cause it does not know.
Ruling R250 on its points: (a) **I1 and I2 — one Zotero library is read by one import or sync at a time in the open library, whatever the project, and the claim is taken before the first wait.** The service holds ONE place that says which Zotero libraries are taken (by canonical ref): a manual Web import's start and resume, and the holder for each link before it starts, check it and claim it in the same synchronous step (no await between a successful check and the claim); the claim is not given up before its batch is registered as running and never outlives its batch or run (a start that fails gives it up; a paused or suspended batch holds none). The loser gets the existing sentences ("Another import from this Zotero library is running." / the link's record `import-running`). Tests with requests sent together, in both orders, and for the other-project direction; and two manual imports of one Zotero library sent together make one batch. A numeric library id is brought to its canonical form in the import route before it is used for anything ("042" is library 42). (b) **I3 — the run in progress is left out of the history's answer** (the state's `running` and the events say it). (c) **I4 — a line names a cause only when its record shows that cause.** With no counts: "Synced with Zotero at 14:32 today, with problems. Unlock the project to see what they were."; the attached-files sentence only when the record shows that attached files failed; every way a run becomes "finished with problems" is mapped to its sentence, with a test per way. (d) M1: the undo's answer says "paused" only when a link was in fact paused (`pauseLink` may return how many it changed — the one change allowed in the library). (e) M2, the words for the counts in a status line, each only when not zero, in this order: "N new", "N changed", "N waiting for your review" (the plan's), then "N changed in Zotero and here (waiting for your decision)", "N deleted or binned in Zotero (kept here)", "N back in Zotero", "N cleared in Zotero (kept here)". (f) M3: after a batch id, "Some items had already come in." (true for a resumed batch too); an outcome this build does not know is said as "A sync at <time> was recorded by a newer version of this app." and nothing more. (g) M4: the shutdown test must fail when the holder's stop is taken out of the close; "nothing runs by itself" is tested with the clock moved a day on, not with a short wait; a test sends two runs together. (h) M5 accepted as built: a key check clears pauses in the open library only (the key is per computer; another library's pause is shown, with its remedy, when that library is open) — Z9 says the remedy on the row. (i) M6 accepted (the implementer's choice, the reviewer agrees): a successful key check also clears "library gone" where the key reads that library again. (j) M7: the holder ends every link's run with a "finished" event whatever throws — the runner's record when it returned one (without counts if reading them failed), otherwise a failed record with the reason "error"; if a started record would then be left reading "the app was closed", the report names the smallest library signature that would settle it (not built now). (k) M8: a batch that belongs to a sync cannot be paused or resumed through the import routes — 409 "This import belongs to a Zotero sync. **Defect D26 (found by Z9's implementer, in Z8 as merged): any save of the sync settings turns every undone library's syncing back on.** The settings route clears the pause "undone" on every link at every save (`routes/zotero-sync.ts`, the save's `keep`), so pressing "Save" after changing "How often", or "Turn syncing on again" on one row, resumes every library whose last sync the researcher undid — and the next sync brings back what they undid. The sentence on the screen promises the opposite ("paused until you turn it on again on this screen").
Ruling R265: (a) **D26 — the pause "undone" is cleared only for a link the request names**: `ZoteroSyncSettingsRequest.links[i]` gains an optional `resume: true`; with it, that link's "undone" is cleared by the save; without it, an existing "undone" is KEPT whatever else the save changes (the project, the options, the automatic settings); any other value is refused naming the field; "key refused" and "library gone" are never cleared by a save, as now; a library unlinked and linked again is a new link without a pause. The screen's "Turn syncing on again" on a row sends the service's last confirmed settings with `resume: true` for that row only (never the form's unsaved draft); "Save" sends no `resume`. Built by Z9's implementer in Z9's branch (the service's route and shared type are opened to it for exactly this), reviewed with Z9. (b) **Commit trailers — the coordinator's own slip:** the project's rule (CLAUDE.md) is that a commit carries the trailers its own session's instructions give; since about 23:30 UTC the coordinator's dispatches to Opus and Sonnet agents (F6, F10, W11, Z9, and the small rounds and the merge in Z8's, F10's and F6's worktrees) told them to write the coordinator's own Co-Authored-By line instead. Z9's implementer rightly used its own. Decided: Z9's commits stand; from now on dispatches say "the trailers your own session's instructions give" and nothing more; the commits already made with the coordinator's line (the session link in them is right; only the model named in Co-Authored-By is the coordinator's rather than the agent's) are left as they are — two of them are already on the pushed reader branch — and the owner is told.
